I don’t want to overstate it, but that’s honestly why I came in. I just can’t seem to shake this heavy, hopeless feeling, like everything I built back up after the divorce has collapsed, and I’m just… sitting under it most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Over the past month, have you had much less interest or pleasure in the things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… maybe a little, but I don’t want to say it’s completely gone. I still sort of want to enjoy things, if that makes sense. I just haven’t really been doing much, or when I do, it doesn’t feel the same. Is that still what you mean by less interest?\n\nDoctor: Over the past month, have you been feeling worthless or blaming yourself a lot?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to sound like I’m being overly harsh or dramatic, but yes. A lot.\n\nI keep telling myself I’m a terrible engineer, like every missed calculation or tense email is evidence that I shouldn’t be trusted. And personally… I keep thinking I failed as a wife, and that I’m just a burden to people. I know that probably sounds extreme, but it’s hard not to replay everything and feel like I’m the common problem.\n\nDoctor: Before this past month, have you ever had another period lasting at least two weeks when you felt depressed or lost interest in things most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I… honestly don’t know. I’ve had rough patches before, like anyone, but I’m not sure I’d say it was most of the day nearly every day for two full weeks. I don’t want to answer wrong or make it sound bigger than it was.\n\nDoctor: Over the past month, have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to answer wrong or make it sound bigger than it was.\n\nDoctor: Over the past month, have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I keep wanting to say it’s just stress, but it’s been pretty bad.\n\nI’ll look at a basic drainage calculation or a spreadsheet I’ve used for years and my mind just kind of blanks. Then I sit there rereading the same numbers over and over, and I still don’t trust myself to make a decision. That counts as trouble concentrating, right?\n\nDoctor: Over the past month, has your appetite changed much?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything.\n\nI think it’s been… maybe a little off? Some days I don’t feel very hungry, but I don’t know if it’s enough to call it a big change. I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Over the past month, have you had thoughts that you would be better off dead or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… yeah. I’m sorry, that’s hard to say.\n\nI don’t think I’ve been, like, trying to do anything, but I have had thoughts like… it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And then even when there isn’t anything useful left to do, I’ll pace around my apartment like I’m still supposed to be doing something.\n\nDoes that count as what you mean by restless?\n\nDoctor: During these past few weeks, have you had times when you felt unusually energized or unusually confident, even while feeling low?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, not in a way I’m proud of, exactly.\n\nBefore I sort of crashed, there were these stretches where I felt really wired, like everything was finally clicking and I was unusually sure of myself. At the time I told myself it was just me being productive or confident.\n\nBut I was also… more irritable than I want to admit. I snapped at people in meetings over little comments that normally wouldn’t bother me. I know that probably doesn’t sound great. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when your mood was abnormally elevated or irritable for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but before this crash there were several days where I felt kind of weirdly wired, like everything was finally clicking and I was really sure of myself.\n\nAnd then other times it wasn’t exactly “happy” elevated, more like I was irritable. I’d snap at people in meetings over tiny comments that normally wouldn’t bother me.
